y_{a,b,c}^{\pm,j}\leq y_{q}^{\pm,j}
. Indeed, the difference
y_{q}^{+,j}-y_{a,b,c}^{+,j}
would be small (as the increase in curvature (
3.19
) would be used on a fairly short interval), and insufficient to overcome the difference
y_{q^{\prime}}^{+,j}-y_{q}^{+,j}
.

To address this, we will effectively “induct on
\mathfrak{d}
”; in particular, we will first show that
H_{t}(z)
reduces when
\mathtt{d}_{z}\sim\bm{d}
, and then decrement
\mathtt{d}_{z}
by factors of
N^{\delta}
until it is close to
\mathfrak{d}
. For a given value of
\bar{\mathtt{d}}=\mathtt{d}_{z}
, we will apply Proposition
3.26
with the
(s_{0},s_{+})
approximately equal to
(\bar{\mathtt{d}},N^{B\delta/9}\bar{\mathtt{d}})
here. After the first several decrements (where
\bar{\mathtt{d}}\approx\bm{d}
, so the arguments are similar to in the
\mathfrak{d}=\bm{d}
case), the right endpoints of these cells will by the inductive hypothesis be in regions where we have already reduced the height of
(H_{t})
to
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
(up a small additive error). In this way, we will only have to compare
\mathcal{H}_{a,b,c}
to
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
(and not to
\mathcal{H}_{q}
), for which the smaller length scale of
N^{B\delta/9}\bar{\mathtt{d}}
of the interval will suffice.

The next proposition, which quickly implies Proposition
7.6
, provides this inductive statement. It indicates how long it takes for
H_{t}(z)
to decrease to nearly
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z)
, depending on the value of
\mathtt{d}_{z}
.

Proposition 8.14
.

For any integer
\mathfrak{K}\in[1,\delta^{-1}]
and time
t\in[q^{-1}\delta N^{1+10B\delta}\omega\mathfrak{K},N^{5}]
, w.o.p. the following holds. We have that
H_{t}(z)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z)+\mathfrak{K}N^{\delta/2-1}
, for each
z\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}
satisfying
\mathtt{d}_{z}\geq L^{-2}N^{1-\mathfrak{K}\delta}\mathfrak{d}
.

Proof of
7.6
if
\mathfrak{d}<\bm{d}
.

This follows from taking
\mathfrak{K}=\delta^{-1}
in the second statement of Proposition
8.14
.
∎

To show Proposition
8.14
, we induct on
\mathfrak{K}
, the statement being true at
\mathfrak{K}=1
,
as then there does not exist any
z\in\mathfrak{X}
for which
\mathtt{d}_{z}\geq L^{-2}N^{1-\mathfrak{K}\delta}\mathfrak{d}
(as
L^{-2}N^{1-\mathfrak{K}\delta}\mathfrak{d}\gg 1
, since
\mathfrak{d}\geq N^{-1/2}
). So, assume that Proposition
8.14
holds at
\mathfrak{K}-1
, and we will show it at
\mathfrak{K}
.

Throughout, we fix
a point
\bar{z}=(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}
satisfying
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq L^{-2}N^{1-\mathfrak{K}\delta}\mathfrak{d}
. We further set
\mathsf{T}=q^{-1}\delta N^{1+10B\delta}\omega\mathfrak{K}
, which is the lower bound for the time interval considered in Proposition
8.14
. Then we must show that, w.o.p., for all
t\in[\mathsf{T},N^{5}]
we have

(8.18)

\displaystyle H_{t}(\bar{z})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})+\mathfrak{K}N^{\delta/2-1}.

To that end, we may assume that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}<L^{-2}N^{1-(\mathfrak{K}-1)\delta}\mathfrak{d}
, as otherwise these results follow from the inductive hypothesis given by the second part of Proposition
8.14
(at
\mathfrak{K}-1
). Thus, we have

(8.19)

\displaystyle L^{-2}N^{1-\mathfrak{K}\delta}\mathfrak{d}\leq\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\leq L^{-2}N^{1-(\mathfrak{K}-1)\delta}\mathfrak{d}.

We can also assume by symmetry that

(8.20)

\displaystyle\bar{x}\geq x_{q}^{SW}.

8.2.2.
Properties of cells and the
k
-th level line

To show (
8.18
), we follow the reasoning in Section
8
, with the
\mathfrak{d}
there replaced by
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
here. We first define the analog of
k
from
Definition

8.3
(it will be larger here than there, which will eventually be a convenience to compare level lines). As in
Definition

3.22
, let
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
denote the
(k/N)
-level line of
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
, where

(8.21)

\frac{k}{N}=\frac{N^{5R\delta}}{\omega^{3}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}}.

Observe by (
7.7
) (and the fact that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq L^{-2}\mathfrak{d}
) that

(8.22)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{k}{N}\leq N^{(5R+6A)\delta}\cdot\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{3}}{\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}}\leq L^{2}N^{11R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{2},

meaning that
k/N
is not too much larger than
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{2}
. Also observe that

(8.23)

\displaystyle N^{(A+4R)\delta-1}\ll kN^{-1}\leq L^{2}N^{5R\delta}(\mathfrak{d}\omega^{3})^{-1}\ll N^{B\delta}(\mathfrak{d}\omega^{3})^{-1}\ll\bm{d}^{2},

where the first and second bounds hold since
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq L^{-2}\mathfrak{d}\geq L^{-2}N^{-1/2}
; the third holds since
B\geq 10R
; and the fourth holds by the fact that
\bm{d}=L^{10}N^{B\delta}\omega^{1/2}(qN)^{-1/2}
(by (
7.8
)) and the two lower bounds in (
7.7
) (with the fact that
B\geq 10A
).

Hence, the curve
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q^{\prime}}
intersects the arctic boundary
\mathfrak{A}_{q^{\prime}}
within
\mathcal{Y}^{-}
, at a point
\mathfrak{w}_{0}=(\mathfrak{l}_{q^{\prime}}(k/N),k/N)
with
\mathfrak{l}_{q^{\prime}}(k/N)<x_{q^{\prime}}^{SW}
, such that
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q^{\prime}}(x)=k/N
for
x\leq\mathfrak{l}_{q^{\prime}}(k/N)
(recall
3.23
). Note that
\mathfrak{w}_{0}\in\mathfrak{F}^{SW}_{q^{\prime}}
, the frozen region defined in
Definition

3.8
.

We next define the analog of the coordinates
x^{\pm}
from
Definition

8.3
.

Definition 8.15
.

Let
{\bf L}^{\pm}
denote the line parallel to the
y
-axis and with
x
-coordinate
x^{\pm}
, defined by

\displaystyle x^{-}=\mathfrak{l}_{q^{\prime}}(kN^{-1});\qquad x^{+}=\bar{x}+N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}.

Observe by Definition
7.3
, with the fact that
\bar{z}=(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}
, that
\mathbf{L}^{-}
and
\mathbf{L}^{+}
intersect the cell
\mathcal{Y}
. Let
\bf S
denote the strip between
\bf L^{-}
and
\bf L^{+}
. Given
z\in\mathcal{Y}
, also let
{\bf L}^{z}
denote the line parallel to the
y
-axis and containing
z
.

Let us make two reductions in showing the two statements above at
\bar{z}
.

Lemma 8.16
.

Proposition
8.14
holds under either of the following two conditions.

(1)

The point
\bar{z}
is above the level line
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{kN^{-1-4R\delta}}
(namely,
\bar{y}\geq\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{kN^{-1-4R\delta}}(\bar{x})
).

(2)

We have
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}<N^{-R\delta-1}(q-q^{\prime})^{-1}
(which holds if
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}<N^{-2R\delta}\omega^{-1}
).

Proof.

To show the first statement, suppose that
\bar{z}
is above the level line
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{kN^{-1-4R\delta}}
. Then we would have that
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})\geq kN^{-1-4R\delta}\gg N^{A\delta-1}
by (
8.23
) and that
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\gtrsim(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z}))^{1/3}\geq(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}kN^{-1-4R\delta})^{1/3}=(N^{R\delta}\omega^{-3})^{1/3}\gg\omega^{-1}
, by Lemma
3.18
and (
8.21
). As such, the ceiling constraint on the Glauber dynamics
(H_{s})
would then imply that
H_{t}(\bar{z})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})
, thereby showing Proposition
8.14
.

To show the second, observe that
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}<N^{-R\delta-1}(q-q^{\prime})^{-1}
holds if
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}<N^{-2R\delta}\omega^{-1}
does, by (
7.16
). Now suppose that
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}<N^{-R\delta-1}(q-q^{\prime})^{-1}
. If
q-q^{\prime}\leq N^{R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q}
, then we would have that

\displaystyle H_{t}(\bar{z})-\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q}(\bar{z})-\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})\lesssim N^{R\delta}(q-q^{\prime})(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\leq N^{-1},

by the ceiling constraint on
(H_{s})
and Lemma
3.21
. This then yields Proposition
8.14
.

So, assume instead that
q-q^{\prime}>N^{R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q}
(and
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}<N^{-R\delta-1}(q-q^{\prime})^{-1}
), so that
q-q^{\prime}>N^{R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
(since
\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q}\geq\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
for
\bar{x}\geq x_{q}^{SW}
and
q\geq q^{\prime}
). In this case, let

z^{\prime}=(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
be above
\bar{z}
(meaning that
x^{\prime}=\bar{x}
and
y^{\prime}\geq\bar{y}
) so that
(\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}=\max\{\omega^{-1},N^{R\delta}(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\}
. Then,
H(z^{\prime})\geq H(\bar{z})
for any admissible height function
H
. We also have that
\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\geq\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
and
\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}}\geq\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
.
Hence,
\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\geq N^{R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\geq N^{R\delta-1}\geq N^{A\delta-1}
. As such, the ceiling constraint of
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
applies at
z^{\prime}
, that is,
H_{t}(z^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})
.

We also have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})-\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})\lesssim\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\leq N^{2R\delta-3}(q-q^{\prime})^{-3}\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}^{-1}<N^{-3}(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{3/2})^{-2}\lesssim N^{-1},

where the first statement holds by Lemma
3.18
; the second by the fact that
(\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}=\max\{\omega^{-1},N^{R\delta}(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\}\leq N^{R\delta-1}(q-q^{\prime})^{-1}
; the third by the fact that
q-q^{\prime}>N^{R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
(and that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\leq\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}
); and the fourth from the fact that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\geq N^{-1}
. Hence,
H_{t}(\bar{z})\leq H_{t}(z^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})+N^{\delta/2-1}
, which again yields Proposition
8.14
.
∎

8.2.3.
Comparison of level lines

In view of Lemma
8.16
, for the remainder of this proof, we will assume that
\bar{z}
lies below
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N^{1+4R\delta}}
, and that
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\geq N^{-R\delta-1}(q-q^{\prime})^{-1}\geq N^{-2R\delta-1}\omega^{-1}
. Next, given
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
, let
s_{x}:=x-\mathfrak{l}_{q^{\prime}}(k/N)
, and denote

\displaystyle s_{0}=s_{\bar{x}};\qquad s_{+}=s_{x^{+}};\qquad s_{-}=\max\{s_{x^{-}},0\}.

The following is analogous to
8.6
and further provides an estimate on
s_{0}
.

Lemma 8.17
.

The assumptions of
3.26
are verified, with
q,\varepsilon,h_{0}
there equal to
q^{\prime},\varepsilon_{0},(k/N)
here. Moreover,

(8.24)

\displaystyle\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\lesssim s_{0}\lesssim N^{8R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}.

Proof.

To show (
8.24
), let
\bar{z}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
denote the point with
x
-coordinate
\bar{x}
, so that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}^{\prime}}\asymp s_{\bar{x}}
. Then, by Lemma
8.16
,
\bar{z}
is below
\bar{z}^{\prime}
, so
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\leq\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}^{\prime}}\asymp s_{\bar{x}}
. This shows the first bound in (
8.24
); to confirm the second observe that, if
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}^{\prime}}\geq 2\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
, then
\mathfrak{e}_{\bar{z}^{\prime};q^{\prime}}\gtrsim\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
(by the convexity of the arctic boundary from Lemma
3.10
). Therefore, either
s_{\bar{x}}\asymp\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}^{\prime}}<2\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
or
\mathfrak{e}_{\bar{z}^{\prime};q^{\prime}}\gtrsim\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}^{\prime}}
. The second bound of (
8.24
) holds in the first case. In the latter, we have
\mathfrak{e}_{\bar{z}^{\prime};q^{\prime}}\gtrsim\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}^{\prime}}\asymp s_{\bar{x}}
, so

\displaystyle\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}s_{\bar{x}}^{3/2}\lesssim\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}^{\prime}}^{1/2}\mathfrak{e}_{\bar{z}^{\prime};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\asymp\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z}^{\prime})=kN^{-1}\lesssim L^{2}N^{11R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{2},

by Lemma
3.18
and (
8.22
). This again verifies the second bound in (
8.24
).

The proof of the first statement of the lemma is similar to that of
8.6
, and it differs only in the way the upper bound in (
3.16
) is checked. In the present case, note by
Definition

8.15
and (
8.24
) that
s^{+}-s^{-}=x^{+}-x^{-}=2N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\lesssim N^{B\delta/9}s_{0}
.
On the other hand,
s_{0}\lesssim N^{-B\delta/4}
(by Definition
7.3
for the cell
\mathcal{Y}^{-}
and (
8.17
)), and this implies
N^{B\delta/9}s_{0}\ll s_{0}^{1/2}
as desired.
∎

By
3.26
,
we can then find
a,b,c\in[1-C\varepsilon_{0},1+C\varepsilon_{0}]

and
\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}
such that the statements of the proposition hold.
In analogy with the case
\mathfrak{d}={\bm{d}}
, we will assume to simplify formulas that
\mathfrak{r}=\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}=0
; see
8.7
.
The following is analogous to
Definition

8.8
. The index shift by
\sigma
below is to account for the fact that
H_{t}(w)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(w)+(\mathfrak{K}-1)N^{\delta/2-1}
when
\mathtt{d}_{w}\geq L^{-2}N^{1-\mathfrak{K}\delta}\mathfrak{d}
, by the inductive hypothesis. The vertical shift by
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2}N^{\delta/4-1}
is a convenience to compare level lines (particularly in the third part of Lemma
8.21
); observe that such a shift only affects the height function
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
at
\bar{z}
by a quantity of order
N^{\delta/4-1}\ll N^{\delta/2-1}
.

Definition 8.18
.

Set

\displaystyle\sigma=\sigma_{\mathfrak{K}}=\lfloor(\mathfrak{K}-1)N^{\delta/2}\rfloor.

In the following, to lighten notation, we drop the floors. For all
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
and
j\in[1,k+\sigma]
, let

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{j}(x):=\mathcal{U}^{(j-\sigma)/N}_{a,b,c}(x)-\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2}N^{\delta/4-1},

where
\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{r}=-\infty
if
r<0
. Further let
y_{q}^{\pm,j}=\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N}(x^{\pm})
and
y^{\pm,j}_{a,b,c}=\widetilde{U}_{j}(x^{\pm})
.

Now, in addition to the inductive procedure explained in the beginning of Section
8.2
, a second difference between the cases
\mathfrak{d}=\bm{d}
and
\mathfrak{d}<\bm{d}
is as follows. When
\mathfrak{d}=\bm{d}
, we had
H_{t}\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
along the
k/N
-level line
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
(see Remark
8.5
). Now when
\mathfrak{d}<\bm{d}
, this will not necessarily be the case; it may occur
(\mathtt{d}_{w}\mathtt{e}_{w;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}<\omega
for some points
w\in\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
(as, unlike in Lemma
8.2
,
\mathtt{d}_{w}
is now not of constant order for
w\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}
). At such points the ceiling constraint
H_{t}(w)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(w)
must be replaced by the weaker one
H_{t}(w)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q}(w)
.

To address this, the following definition gives a “transition point”
\mathfrak{z}\in\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
, where the ceiling constraint changes from
\mathcal{H}_{q}
to
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
.

Definition 8.19
.

Let
\mathfrak{z}=(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{y})
denote the rightmost point on
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{Y}
with

(\mathtt{d}_{\mathfrak{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\mathfrak{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\leq\omega^{-1}
, if such a point exists. Otherwise, set
\mathfrak{z}:=\mathfrak{w}_{0}
(defined above Definition
8.15
).

The next lemma provides some properties of
\mathfrak{z}
. The relevance of the case
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}<N^{2R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
considered in its third statement will be explained above Lemma
8.21
.

Lemma 8.20
.

The following statements hold.

(1)

If
\mathfrak{z}\neq\mathfrak{w}_{0}
, then
\mathfrak{p}<\bar{x}
(that is,
\mathfrak{z}
is to the left of
\bar{z}
).

(2)

If
\mathfrak{z}\neq\mathfrak{w}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{p}\geq x^{SW}_{q}
(that is,
\mathfrak{z}
is to the right of the southwest tangency location), then
\mathtt{d}_{\mathfrak{z}}\leq N^{-4R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
,

(3)

If
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}<N^{2R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
, then
\mathtt{d}_{w}\geq N^{R\delta/4}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
holds for all
w=(x,y)\in\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
with
x\in[x^{-},\mathfrak{p}]
(that is, to the left of
\mathfrak{z}
).

Proof.

To show the first statement of the lemma, let
z^{\prime}=(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
denote any point on the
kN^{-1}
-level line with
x^{\prime}\geq\bar{x}
(namely, to the right of
\bar{z}
). Then,

\displaystyle(\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\asymp(\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z^{\prime}))^{1/3}\geq(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}kN^{-1})^{1/3}\geq N^{5R\delta/3}\omega^{-1},

where the first statement holds by Lemma
3.18
; the second since
\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\geq\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
(as
z^{\prime}
is to the right of
\bar{z}
, and
\bar{z}
is below
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
by Lemma
8.16
); and the third by (
8.21
). Hence,
(\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}>\omega^{-1}
, so
z^{\prime}\neq\mathfrak{z}
, meaning that
\mathfrak{z}
must lie to the left of
\bar{z}
.

To show the second statement of the lemma, observe that if
\mathfrak{z}
exists and satisfies
\mathfrak{p}\geq x^{SW}_{q}
, then by
3.18
and (
8.21
) we have

(8.25)

\displaystyle\mathtt{d}_{\mathfrak{z}}\asymp(\mathtt{d}_{\mathfrak{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\mathfrak{z};q^{\prime}})^{3/2}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{z})^{-1}=\omega^{-3}k^{-1}N=N^{-5R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}.

To show the third statement of the lemma, suppose that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}<N^{2R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
.
Observe by Lemma
3.18
that we either have
kN^{-1}=\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(w)\asymp\mathtt{d}_{w}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{w;q^{\prime}}^{3/2}
(if
x\geq x_{q^{\prime}}^{SW}
) or that
kN^{-1}=\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(w)\asymp\mathtt{d}_{w}^{2}
(if
x<x_{q^{\prime}}^{SW}
). In either case, we have
\mathtt{d}_{w}^{2}\gtrsim kN^{-1}
. So,
\mathtt{d}_{w}^{2}\gtrsim kN^{-1}\geq N^{4R\delta}\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})\gtrsim N^{R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{2}
, where the second inequality holds by Lemma
8.16
and the third holds by Lemma
3.18
, with the fact that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{3/2}\leq N^{3R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}
. This shows the lemma.
∎

The next lemma is an analog of Lemma
8.9
. Observe that its third part compares
\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
to
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}(x)
for all
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
, while its fourth compares
\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
to
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)
to the left of
\mathfrak{z}
, where the ceiling might be
\mathcal{H}_{q}
, when
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq N^{2R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
. If instead
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}<N^{2R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
, we might no longer have that
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k+\sigma}(x)
for
x\in[x^{-},\mathfrak{p}]
. However, in this case, the third part of Lemma
8.20
gives
\mathtt{d}_{\mathfrak{z}}\geq N^{R\delta/4}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\gg N^{\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
. So we will instead use the inductive hypothesis, (
8.18
) at
\mathfrak{K}-1
, to obtain the improved ceiling of
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
(up to a small additive error) also to the left of
\mathfrak{z}
(see the proof of Lemma
8.23
below).

Lemma 8.21
.

The following four statements hold, for some constant
c>0
.

(1)

For each
1-\sigma\leq j\leq k
, we have
y^{+,j}_{q^{\prime}}\geq y^{+,j+\sigma}_{a,b,c}+cq\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}(x^{+}-\bar{x})^{2}
.

(2)

For each
1-\sigma\leq j\leq k
, we have
y^{-,j}_{q}\geq y^{-,j+\sigma}_{a,b,c}
.

(3)

We have
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q^{\prime}}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k+\sigma}(x)
for all
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
.

(4)

If
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq N^{2R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
, then
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k+\sigma}(x)
for
x\in[x^{-},\mathfrak{p}]
(to the left of
\mathfrak{z}
).

Proof.

Let us first show
y^{+,j}_{q}\geq y^{+,j}_{a,b,c}+cq\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}(x^{+}-\bar{x})^{2}
. To that end, we have that

(8.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}(x^{+})-\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{k/N}(x^{+})\geq C^{-1}q\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}(x^{+}-\bar{x})^{2},

where we used the facts that
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}(x)-\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{k/N}(x)\gtrsim q\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}(x-\bar{x})^{2}
for
x\in[\bar{x},x^{+}]
(by (
3.19
)); that
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}(\bar{x})=\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{k/N}(\bar{x})
(by (
3.18
)); that
\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}(\bar{x})=\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{k/N}(\bar{x})
(by (
3.17
)); and that
s_{0}\gtrsim\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
by (
8.24
). Since
s_{x^{+}}=s_{\bar{x}}+N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
and
s_{\bar{x}}\lesssim N^{8R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\ll N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
by (
8.24
), we have that
s_{x^{+}}\asymp x^{+}-\bar{x}
. Together with (
3.22
), this gives

(8.27)

\displaystyle\big|\big(\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}(x^{+})-\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{k/N}(x^{+})\big)-\big(\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{j/N}(x^{+})-\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{j/N}(x^{+})\big)\big|\leq Cq(x^{+}-\bar{x})^{5/3}(kN^{-1})^{2/3}.

We moreover have
kN^{-1}\leq L^{2}N^{11R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{2}
by (
8.22
) and that
x^{+}-\bar{x}=N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
. Combining these estimates yields
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}(x^{+}-\bar{x})^{1/3}\gg N^{(B/28-22R)\delta}(k/N)^{2/3}
. As such, for
B\geq 650R
, it follows that
qs_{0}(x^{+}-\bar{x})^{2}\gg q(x^{+}-\bar{x})^{5/3}(k/N)^{2/3}
. Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle y_{q^{\prime}}^{+,j}=\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{j/N}(x^{+})>\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{j/N}(x^{+})+N^{B\delta/10}q\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{3}\geq y_{a,b,c}^{+,j}+(2C)^{-1}q\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}(x^{+}-\bar{x})^{2},

which confirms the first part of the lemma (upon recalling Definition
8.18
).

We next show the second part of the lemma. From
Item

1
and

Item

2
of
3.26
, the curve

\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q^{\prime}}
is above
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{a,b,c}
.
Furthermore, both
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}
intersect the vertical line
\mathbf{L}^{-}
at the same point

(x^{-},k/N)\in\mathfrak{F}^{SW}_{q}
. Thus,

y^{-,k}_{q}=kN^{-1}\geq y^{-,k}_{a,b,c}
.
Now, again by the fact that the left
endpoints of
\mathcal{U}^{j/N}_{q}
, for all
j\leq k
, are in
\mathfrak{F}^{SW}_{q}
, we have
y^{-,k}_{q}-y^{-,j}_{q}=(k-j)N^{-1}
.
On the other hand,
y^{-,k}_{a,b,c}-y^{-,j}_{a,b,c}\geq(k-j)N^{-1}
(as the gradient of the height function is in the triangle
\mathcal{T}
in (
3.3
)), which implies that
y^{-,j}_{q}\geq y^{-,j}_{a,b,c}

for every
j\leq k
.

To show the third part of the lemma observe that, since
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
is above
\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{k/N}
, by Definition
8.18
we have for all
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
that

(8.28)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}(x)-\widetilde{U}_{k+\sigma}(x)\geq\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2}N^{\delta/4-1}\geq 0.

To show the fourth part of the lemma, we may assume
\mathfrak{z}\neq\mathfrak{w}_{0}
. By (
8.28
), we must then verify that

\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}(x)-\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2}N^{\delta/4-1}
.
Let
w=(x,y)
and
w^{\prime}=(x,y^{\prime})
be points on

\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}
and
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
, respectively, with the same
x
-coordinate. We must then
show

(8.29)

\displaystyle|y-y^{\prime}|\leq\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2}N^{\delta/4-1}.

Consider first the case when
x\in[x^{SW}_{q},\mathfrak{z}]
, if this interval is nonempty. By
3.21
and the second part of Lemma
3.18
,

|y-y^{\prime}|\asymp\mathtt{d}_{w}(q-q^{\prime})
. On the other hand,
\mathtt{d}_{w}
is increasing along the curve

\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}
: this follows from the fact that the gradient of
height functions belongs to the triangle
\mathcal{T}
defined in
(
3.3
), so that the vertical coordinate of any level line is
increasing as a function of the horizontal coordinate. This, together with the second statement of Lemma
8.20
and Lemma
8.16
, yields

\displaystyle|y-y^{\prime}|\lesssim(q-q^{\prime})\mathtt{d}_{\mathfrak{z}}\lesssim N^{-4R\delta}(q-q^{\prime})\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\ll N^{-1}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2},

which shows (
8.29
).

If instead
x\in[x^{-},\min(x^{SW}_{q},\mathfrak{p})]
, then
|\mathcal{H}_{q}(w)-\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(w)|\leq|q-q^{\prime}|(\mathfrak{d}_{w}\mathtt{e}_{w;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}
(by
3.21
). Together with the fact that
\partial_{y}\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z)\asymp 1
for
z
to the left of
x^{SW}_{q}
(see the fourth part of
3.18
), this yields
|y-y^{\prime}|\lesssim|q-q^{\prime}|(\mathtt{d}_{w}\mathtt{e}_{w;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\lesssim|q-q^{\prime}|(\mathtt{d}_{\mathfrak{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\mathfrak{z};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}=|q-q^{\prime}|\omega^{-1}

where the last bound holds because
w
is to the left of
\mathfrak{z}
(and
\mathtt{d}_{w}
and
\mathtt{e}_{w;q^{\prime}}
are increasing in
x
near the left edge of the arctic boundary, due to its convexity).
By (
7.16
), we obtain that
|y-y^{\prime}|\lesssim N^{R\delta-1}
, so (
8.29
) follows if
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq N^{2R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
.
∎

8.2.4.
Proof of
7.6
if
\mathfrak{d}<\bm{d}

The next definition prescribes an analog of Definition
8.18
, now with respect to the discrete (random) height function
H_{t}
. Below,
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}=\mathsf{T}-q^{-1}\delta N^{1+10B\delta}\omega
is chosen so that the inductive hypothesis, given by the
\mathfrak{K}-1
case of (
8.18
), holds for all
t\in[\mathsf{T}^{\prime},N^{5}]
.

Definition 8.22
.

Set
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}=q^{-1}\delta N^{1+10B\delta}\omega(\mathfrak{K}-1)
. For each integer
j\in[1,k]
and real number
t\in[\mathsf{T}^{\prime},N^{5}]
, let
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x)
denote the
jN^{-1}
-level line (as in Definition
2.6
, recall also
Footnote

11
) of
H_{t}-\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2}N^{\delta/4-1}
, and set
\mathtt{y}_{t}^{\pm,j}=\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x^{\pm})
.

The following lemma indicates that the endpoints and curves from Definition
8.22
lie above the ones associated with
\mathcal{H}_{a,b,c}
from Definition
8.18
. In the case when
\mathfrak{d}=\bm{d}
, it was a quick consequence of Lemma
8.9
, with the ceiling constraint on
(H_{s})
.

Lemma 8.23
.

We have w.o.p. that the following statements hold for all
t\in[\mathsf{T}^{\prime},N^{5}]
.

(1)

We have that
y_{a,b,c}^{\pm,j}\leq\mathsf{y}_{t}^{\pm,j}
for each
j\in[1,k]
.

(2)

We have that
\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)\leq\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)
for each
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
.

Proof.

By the ceiling constraint of
\mathcal{H}_{q}
on
(H_{s})_{s\geq 0}
, we have that
\mathsf{y}_{t}^{-,j}=\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x^{-})\geq y_{q}^{-,j}
. This, together with the second part of Lemma
8.21
, implies that
\mathsf{y}_{t}^{-,j}\geq y_{a,b,c}^{-,j}
. Thus, to verify the first part of the lemma, it remains to show that
\mathsf{y}_{t}^{+,j}\geq y_{a,b,c}^{+,j}
.

To do so, we separately consider two cases. The first is if
y_{a,b,c}^{+,j}\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}
for each
j\in[1,k]
. By Definition
7.3
, this is equivalent to imposing that
y_{a,b,c}^{+,k}\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}
. Then, since
x^{+}=\bar{x}+N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq x^{SW}_{q}+N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
by Definition
8.15
, we have that
\mathtt{d}_{w}\gtrsim N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\gg N^{\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq L^{-2}N^{1-(\mathfrak{K}-1)\delta}\mathfrak{d}
for any
w=(x^{+},y)\in\mathfrak{L}_{q^{\prime}}
. Hence, by the inductive hypothesis (
8.18
) applied at a time
t\in[\mathsf{T}^{\prime},N^{5}]
, we find that
H_{t}(x^{+},y)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(x^{+},y)+(\mathfrak{K}-1)N^{\delta/2-1}
for any
(x^{+},y)\in\mathfrak{L}_{q^{\prime}}
. Recalling that
\sigma=(\mathfrak{K}-1)N^{\delta/2}
, this yields
\mathsf{y}_{t}^{+,j}\geq y_{q^{\prime}}^{+,j-\sigma}
, which together with the first part of Lemma
8.21
yields
\mathsf{y}_{t}^{+,j}\geq y_{a,b,c}^{+,j}
.

Now suppose that
y_{a,b,c}^{+,k}\notin\mathcal{Y}^{-}
, in which case the proof will follow that of Lemma
8.9
. First, we lower bound
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
. Since
y_{a,b,c}^{+,k}\notin\mathcal{Y}^{-}
, we have by Definition
7.3
and (
7.11
) that

(8.30)

\displaystyle x^{+}\geq x^{SW}_{q}+N^{B\delta}\omega^{1/2}(qN)^{-1/2}.

Since
\bar{x}-x^{SW}_{1}\leq s_{\bar{x}}\lesssim N^{8R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
by (
8.24
) and
x^{+}-\bar{x}=s_{x^{+}}-s_{\bar{x}}=N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\gg N^{8R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
, we have that
x^{+}-x^{SW}\asymp N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
. Together with (
8.30
), this gives
s_{+}\asymp x^{+}-\bar{x}
and that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq N^{8B\delta/9}\omega^{1/2}(qN)^{-1/2}
. By the first part of Lemma
8.21
, there thus exists a constant
c>0
such that

(8.31)

\displaystyle y_{q^{\prime}}^{+,j}-y_{a,b,c}^{+,j}\geq y_{q^{\prime}}^{+,j}-y_{a,b,c}^{+,j+\sigma}\geq cqs_{+}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}(x^{+}-\bar{x})\geq cs_{+}N^{B\delta-1}\omega.

We also have that
y_{q^{\prime}}^{+,j}-y_{q}^{+,j}\lesssim(q-q^{\prime})s_{+}\lesssim N^{R\delta-1}s_{+}\omega
, where the first inequality holds by Lemma
3.21
and Lemma
3.18
, and the second holds by (
7.16
). Since
B>R
, this with (
8.31
) implies that
y_{q}^{+,j}\geq y_{a,b,c}^{+,j}
. By the ceiling of
\mathcal{H}_{q}
on
(H_{s})
, we then have that
H_{t}(x^{+},y)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q}(x^{+},y)
. Thus,
\mathsf{y}_{t}^{+,j}\geq y_{q}^{+,j}\geq y_{a,b,c}^{+,j}
, confirming the first part of the lemma.

To show the second, first observe that for
x\in[\mathfrak{p},x^{+}]
we have
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
, where the first inequality holds by the ceiling constraint of
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
on
(H_{s})
for points
(x,y)\in\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
to the right of
\mathfrak{z}
(that is, with
x>\mathfrak{p}
), and the second holds by the third part of Lemma
8.21
(and the ordering of the curves in
\widetilde{U}
). Moreover, if
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq N^{2R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
, we have for
x\in[x^{-},\mathfrak{p}]
that
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
, where the first statement holds by the ceiling constraint of
\mathcal{H}_{q}
on
(H_{s})
, and the second holds by the fourth part of Lemma
8.21
.

Hence, it remains to verify
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
for
x\in[x^{-},\mathfrak{p}]
, when
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}<N^{2R\delta}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}
. In this case, fix
w=(x,y)\in\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
with
x\in[x^{-},\mathfrak{p}]
. By the third part of Lemma
8.20
, we have
\mathtt{d}_{w}\geq N^{R\delta/4}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq N^{\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq L^{-2}N^{1-(\mathfrak{K}-1)\delta}\mathfrak{d}
. Hence, the inductive hypothesis (
8.18
) at
\mathfrak{K}-1
(with the
\bar{z}
there equal to
w
here) applies and yields that we w.o.p. have
H_{t}(w)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(w)+(\mathfrak{K}-1)N^{\delta/2-1}
. Recalling that
\sigma=(\mathfrak{K}-1)N^{\delta/2}
, it follows that
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{(k-\sigma)/N}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
, by the third part of Lemma
8.21
. This establishes the lemma.
∎

The following is analogous to
Definition

8.10
.

Definition 8.24
.

Let
\widehat{U}=\{\widehat{U}_{j}\}_{j<k}
denote the Bernoulli path ensemble with endpoints
(x^{\pm},y^{\pm,j}_{a,b,c})
, conditioned to the event that
\widehat{U}_{k-1}
is weakly below
\widetilde{U}_{k}
and that each
\widehat{U}_{j}
for
j\leq k-1
is weakly below
\mathcal{U}^{(j/N+N^{-1+(B/2)\delta})}_{q^{\prime}}
. Similarly, let
\widecheck{U}=\{\widecheck{U}_{j}\}_{j<k}
denote the Bernoulli path ensemble with endpoints
(x^{\pm},y^{\pm,j}_{a,b,c})
, conditioned to the event that
\widecheck{U}_{k-1}
is weakly below
\widetilde{U}_{k}
.

The following three results are analogs of Lemma
8.11
, Lemma
8.12
, and Lemma
8.13
(with similar proofs).

Lemma 8.25
.

The Bernoulli path ensemble
\widehat{U}
can be coupled with
\widecheck{U}
, so that w.o.p. we have
\widehat{U}=\widecheck{U}
.

Proof of
8.25
.

The proof is similar to that of
8.11
.
The claim follows once we prove

(8.32)

\displaystyle\text{w.o.p., }\widecheck{U}_{j}\text{ is weakly below }\mathcal{U}^{j/N+N^{-1+(B/2)\delta}}_{q^{\prime}},\text{ for every }j\leq k-1.

As in the proof of
8.11
, an application of
3.25
implies that, w.o.p.,
\widecheck{U}_{j}
is below
\mathcal{U}^{j/N+2N^{\delta-1}}_{a,b,c}
and it remains to prove that the latter is below
\mathcal{U}^{j/N+N^{(B/2)\delta-1}}_{q^{\prime}}
. To this purpose, we apply (
3.23
) with
h_{0},\mathfrak{d}_{z}
replaced by
k/N,s
, where
s
is the maximal value of
\mathfrak{d}_{w}
in the part of the strip
\mathbf{S}
below
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N}
. We have

s\lesssim N^{B\delta/9}s_{0}\lesssim N^{(B/9+8R)\delta}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
, where the first inequality follows from
Definition

8.15
defining
x^{+}
and the last follows from (
8.24
). Therefore,
Cq\mathfrak{d}kN^{-1}\lesssim N^{(13R+B/9)\delta}q\omega^{-3}\lesssim N^{(6A+13R+B/9)\delta-1}\lesssim N^{(B/2)\delta-1}
by (
7.7
) and (
7.15
). Then, (
3.23
) implies that
\mathcal{U}^{j/N+2N^{\delta-1}}_{a,b,c}
is below
\mathcal{U}^{j/N+N^{(B/2)\delta-1}}_{q^{\prime}}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.26
.

W.o.p., the following holds for every
1\leq j<k
:
the Bernoulli path
\widehat{U}_{j}
is between
\widetilde{U}_{j-N^{\delta/4}}
and
\widetilde{U}_{j+N^{\delta/4}}
if
j-N^{\delta/4}\geq 0
, or below
\widetilde{U}_{j+N^{\delta/4}}
if
j-N^{\delta/4}<0
.

Proof of
8.26
.

As in the proof of Lemma
8.12
, it suffices by
8.25
to prove the statement for
\widecheck{U}
; this follows from
3.25
.
∎

Lemma 8.27
.

For every
1-\sigma\leq j<k-\sigma
we have
\widetilde{U}_{j+\sigma}(\bar{x})\geq\mathcal{U}^{j/N}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{x})-\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2}N^{\delta/4-1}
.

Proof of
8.27
.

As in the proof of
8.13
, we have
\mathcal{U}^{j/N}_{a,b,c}(\bar{x})\geq\mathcal{U}^{j/N}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{x})
by (
3.18
) and (
3.21
). This, together with Definition
8.18
for
\widetilde{U}_{j}
, implies the lemma.
∎

Finally we can prove
8.14
.
We need a few preliminary definitions.

We let
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}=\mathbf{S}\cap\mathcal{Y}.

Let
G^{+}
denote the ceiling for the dynamics (as imposed by
7.6
), namely,
G^{+}
equals the maximal admissible height function that is at most equal to
\mathcal{H}_{q}
on
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
and at most equal to
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
on
w\in\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
for which
(\mathtt{d}_{w}\mathtt{e}_{w;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\geq\omega^{-1}
and
\mathtt{d}_{w}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{w;q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\geq N^{A\delta-1}
.
Let
H^{+}
denote maximal admissible height function that is at most equal to
G^{+}
on all of
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
; satisfies
H^{+}(x^{\pm},y_{a,b,c}^{\pm,j})\leq j/N
for
j\in[1,k]
; and satisfies
H^{+}(z)\leq k/N
for
z=(x,y)
with
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
and
y=\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
.
Finally, let
H^{-}=\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}-N^{B\delta/2-1}
. Note that the additional constraints (with respect to
G^{+}
) are those imposed by
8.23
.

We have:

Lemma 8.28
.

For every
z\in\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
it holds

(8.33)

\displaystyle H^{+}(z)-H^{-}(z)\leq N^{B\delta-1}.

Proof of
8.28
.

Because of the definition of
H^{-}
, it is enough to show
H^{+}(z)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z)+2N^{6R\delta-1}
.
As explained in the beginning of the proof of Lemma
8.16
, this holds if
z
is above the level line
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{k/N^{1+4R\delta}}
. So, assume that
z
is below this level line. We may also assume that

\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{1/2}\mathfrak{e}_{z;q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\geq N^{A\delta-1}
, as otherwise we could replace
z
by a point
z^{\prime}
vertically above it so that
\mathfrak{d}_{z^{\prime}}^{1/2}\mathfrak{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}=N^{A\delta-1}
; if there we have
H^{+}(z^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})+N^{6R\delta-1}
, then it would follow that
H^{+}(z)\leq H^{+}(z^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})+N^{6R\delta-1}\leq CN^{A\delta-1}+N^{6R\delta-1}\leq 2N^{6R\delta-1}
by (
7.15
) (here,
C
is a positive constant and the third inequality uses
3.18
). Therefore, we assume
\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{1/2}\mathfrak{e}_{z;q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\geq N^{A\delta-1}
. If
(\mathtt{d}_{z}\mathtt{e}_{z;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\geq\omega^{-1}
then
H^{+}(z)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z)
(by definition of
H^{+}
). Therefore, assume in what follows that
(\mathtt{d}_{z}\mathtt{e}_{z;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\leq\omega^{-1}<N^{R\delta-1}(q-q^{\prime})^{-1}
(by (
7.16
)). If
N^{R\delta}(q-q^{\prime})\leq\mathtt{e}_{z;q}
, then we would have that

\displaystyle H^{+}(z)-\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z)\leq\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)-\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z)\lesssim N^{R\delta}(q-q^{\prime})(\mathtt{d}_{z}\mathtt{e}_{z;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\leq N^{2R\delta-1},

by the fact that
H^{+}\leq\mathcal{H}_{q}
and Lemma
3.21
, which establishes the above estimate.

So, assume instead that
N^{R\delta}(q-q^{\prime})>\mathtt{e}_{z;q}
(and
(\mathtt{d}_{z}\mathtt{e}_{z;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}<N^{R\delta-1}(q-q^{\prime})^{-1}
).
As in the proof of Lemma
8.16
, let

z^{\prime}
be above
z
so that
(\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}=\max\{\omega^{-1},N^{R\delta}(\mathtt{d}_{z}\mathtt{e}_{z;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\}
. Then,
H(z^{\prime})\geq H(z)
for any admissible height function
H
. We also have that
\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\geq\mathtt{d}_{{z}}
and
\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}}\geq\mathtt{e}_{{z};q^{\prime}}
.
Hence,
\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\geq N^{R\delta}\mathtt{d}_{{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{{z};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\geq N^{R\delta-1}\geq N^{A\delta-1}
. As such, the ceiling constraint of
\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}
applies at
z^{\prime}
, that is,
H(z^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})
.
We also have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})-\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z)\lesssim\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\leq N^{3R\delta-3}(q-q^{\prime})^{-3}\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}^{-1}<N^{6R\delta-3}(\mathtt{d}_{z}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{z;q^{\prime}}^{3/2})^{-2}\lesssim N^{6R\delta-1},

where the first statement holds by Lemma
3.18
; the second by the fact that
(\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}\mathtt{e}_{z^{\prime};q^{\prime}})^{1/2}=\max\{\omega^{-1},N^{R\delta}(\mathtt{d}_{z}\mathtt{e}_{z;q^{\prime}})^{1/2}\}\leq N^{R\delta-1}(q-q^{\prime})^{-1}
; the third by the fact that
N^{R\delta}(q-q^{\prime})>\mathtt{e}_{z;q^{\prime}}
(and that
\mathtt{d}_{z}\leq\mathtt{d}_{z^{\prime}}
); and the fourth from the fact that
\mathtt{d}_{z}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{z;q^{\prime}}^{3/2}\geq N^{-1}
. Hence,
H(z)\leq H(z^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(z)+N^{6R\delta-1}
, which again (recalling (
7.15
)) yields the above bound.
∎

Proof of
8.14
.

This proof is quite close to that of

7.6
in the case
\mathfrak{d}={\bm{d}}
.
To show
8.14
, we may impose that the height function is above the floor
H^{-}
by monotonicity. Moreover, by
8.23
, w.o.p. we may also impose the ceiling of
H^{+}
on the time interval
[\mathsf{T}^{\prime},N^{5}]
.
By
8.28
,
2.13
and the definition (
7.12
) of the cell
\mathcal{Y}
, the mixing time
T_{\rm mix}^{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}
for the Glauber dynamics on
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
with this ceiling and floor is at most of order
L^{4}N^{2+5B\delta}\bm{d}^{2}(\log N)^{2}\leq\delta q^{-1}N^{8B\delta+1}\omega
for
N
large enough,
where in the last bound we used (
7.8
) in the case
{\bm{d}}>\mathfrak{d}
.
Note that
\mathsf{T}-\mathsf{T}^{\prime}\gtrsim N^{2B\delta}T_{\rm mix}^{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}
. Therefore, by (
2.4
), with overwhelming probability, the dynamics at times in
[\mathsf{T},N^{5}]
can be coupled to
the stationary process constrained between
H^{-}
and
H^{+}
.
Thanks to
2.14
, the claim of
8.14
follows if we prove that the following holds w.o.p. under the uniform measure
\pi
constrained between
H^{-}
and
H^{+}
:

(8.34)

\displaystyle H(\bar{z})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})+\mathfrak{K}N^{\delta/2-1}

(recall that the point
\bar{z}
has been fixed and satisfies the conditions of
8.14
).
Since the event in (
8.34
) is decreasing, and
H^{+}
was defined as the minimum of
G^{+}
with the additional (decreasing) constraints in
8.23
, by monotonicity we can replace
H^{+}
with the maximal height function subject just to the upper constraints in
8.23
. That is, we can replace the level lines (with indices
j\leq k-1
) of the height function under
\pi
by the Bernoulli path ensemble on
[x^{-},x^{+}]
, with left endpoints
(x^{-},y_{a,b,c}^{-,j})
, right endpoints at
(x^{+},y_{a,b,c}^{+,j})
, conditioned to the event that the path with index
k-1
is weakly below
\widetilde{U}_{k}
, and the path with index
j
is weakly below
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{j/N+N^{B\delta/2-1}}
. Note that this is just the ensemble
\widehat{U}
from
Definition

8.24
.
By
8.25
, we may further replace
\widehat{U}
with
\widecheck{U}
, and recall by
8.26
that
\widecheck{U}_{j}
is above
\widetilde{U}_{j-N^{\delta/4}}
. By
8.27
, the latter is above
\mathcal{U}_{q^{\prime}}^{(j-\sigma)/N-N^{\delta/4-1}}-\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2}N^{\delta/4-1}
.
(Recall that
\sigma=(\mathfrak{K}-1)N^{\delta/2}
.)
Wrapping up, we have that w.o.p.

H(\bar{z})\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{x},\bar{y}+\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q^{\prime}}^{-1/2}N^{\delta/4-1})+\sigma/N+N^{\delta/4-1}\leq\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{x},\bar{y})+\sigma/N+N^{\delta/2-1}=\mathcal{H}_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{z})+\mathfrak{K}N^{\delta/2-1},

(where the second inequality follows from the second item in
3.18
, since we are under the assumption that
\bar{x}\geq x^{SW}_{q}\geq x^{SW}_{q^{\prime}}
), thereby confirming (
8.34
).
∎

9.
Proof of
7.7

In this section we show
7.7
. At a first glance, this result may appear qualitatively different from
7.6
, since it states an improved bound (reducing the errors of
N^{R\delta}
to
N^{\delta}
) without an improved assumption. What we will use is that the distance between the
k
-th and
(k+1)
-th level lines of
\mathcal{H}_{q}
decreases as
k
increases (by, for example, the second part of
3.25
). So, we will choose our
k
here to be slightly larger (by a factor of about
N^{8R\delta}
; see (
9.1
)) than the value (
8.5
) of
k
in the proof of Proposition
7.6
. In this way, the error of
N^{R\delta-1}
in the height function around the
k
-th curve will correspond to a fairly small error in the vertical (spatial) direction, of order less than
\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{\delta/2-2/3}
.

This error is negligible from the perspective of a point
\bar{z}\in\mathfrak{X}
, with
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q})^{1/2}\leq N^{R\delta-1}\omega^{-1}
, at which we seek to decrease
(H_{t})
(see (
9.8
)). So, we will be able to vertically shift level lines under consideration by
\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{\delta/2-2/3}
(see (
9.5
) and Definition
9.12
) while minimally affecting the height function around
\bar{z}
, in order to account for the height difference of
N^{R\delta-1}
along the
k
-th level line (see Lemmas
9.3
and
9.13
).

Once this shift is taken into account, the proof of
7.7
will be quite similar to that of
7.6
, and so some arguments in this section will be only outlined. In fact, several aspects in the proof of
7.6
will simplify when showing
7.7
, since the number
k
of curves under consideration here will be fairly small. Indeed, we will have
k\ll N^{C\delta}
for some constant
C>1
, so the height function
(H_{t})
of the Glauber dynamics below the
k
-th curve is at most
N^{C\delta-1}
. This directly enables us to take the essentially minimal value of
H_{\max}\sim N^{C\delta-1}
in
2.13
, without having to implement an auxiliary ceiling constraint (as in the
\widehat{U}
Bernoulli path ensemble from Definitions
8.10
and
8.24
) and then showing that this constraint is irrelevant (as in Lemmas
8.11
and
8.25
).

Throughout this section, we recall from Remark
8.1
that we may assume that the tangency point closest to
w_{0}
is
p^{SW}_{q}
, and that the
x
-coordinate of
w_{0}
is at least
x^{SW}_{q}
(that is,
w_{0}
is to the right of
p_{q}^{SW}
). We further fix a point
\bar{z}=(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}
and let
{\bf L}^{\bar{z}}
be
the vertical line through
\bar{z}
. We then define
k
via

(9.1)

\displaystyle\frac{k}{N}=\frac{N^{30R\delta}}{\omega^{3}\mathfrak{d}}.

Observe by (
7.21
) that
k\leq N^{33R\delta}
.

9.1.
Case I:
{\bm{d}}=\mathfrak{d}

If
\bm{d}=\mathfrak{d}
,
we adopt
Definition

8.3
for
x^{\pm},\bf L^{\pm}
and
\bf S
.
Call

(9.2)

j_{\bar{z}}=\lfloor N\mathcal{H}_{q}(\bar{z})\rfloor+1.

Remark 9.1
.

In the proof of
Item

1
of
7.7
, we can assume that
\bar{z}\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}\cap\mathfrak{L}_{q}
and

(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q})^{1/2}\leq N^{R\delta}\omega^{-1}
.
The assumption
\bar{z}\in\mathfrak{L}_{q}
can be made since the height
is non-decreasing in the vertical direction, and
\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)
in the
r.h.s. of (
7.24
) is zero in
\mathfrak{F}_{q}^{S}
.
In this case, note that (since
\mathfrak{d}_{\bar{z}}\asymp\mathfrak{d}
; see
8.2
)

(9.3)

\displaystyle{j_{\bar{z}}}\asymp N\frac{(\mathfrak{e}_{\bar{z};q}\mathfrak{d}_{\bar{z}})^{3/2}}{\mathfrak{d}}\leq N\frac{(\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}})^{3/2}}{\mathfrak{d}}\lesssim N^{3R\delta}\frac{N}{\omega^{3}\mathfrak{d}}\leq N^{-9R\delta}k.

To prove
Item

2
of
7.7
, we will have
\bar{z}
outside
\mathfrak{L}_{q}
: in this case,

k=\frac{N^{1+30R\delta}}{\omega^{3}\mathfrak{d}}\geq N^{30R\delta}\geq N^{30R\delta}j_{\bar{z}}

using
j_{\bar{z}}=1
and the upper bound on
\omega
in (
7.7
).
In either case, we have then

(9.4)

\displaystyle k\gtrsim N^{8R\delta}j_{\bar{z}}.

9.1.1.
Comparison of level lines

The following lemma is analogous to
8.4
. Its proof is also very similar to that of
8.4
(essentially upon replacing
L^{3}
there with
N^{30R\delta}
here), so we omit it.

Lemma 9.2
.

The intersection
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}\cap\bf S
is contained in
\mathcal{Y}
.

As in the proof of
7.6
, we may apply
3.26
(whose applicability can be proven entirely analogously to as in
8.6
), which implies the existence of
a,b,c,\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}
(here, as in the proof of
7.6
, we drop
\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}
, to lighten notation; see
8.7
) such that (
8.6
) and (
8.7
) hold, and in particular
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{a,b,c}(x),x\in[x^{-},x^{+}].
For

x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
define

(9.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{j}(x)=\mathcal{U}^{j/N}_{a,b,c}(x)-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+\delta/2}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}.

Observe that
\widetilde{U}_{k}
is below
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}
, since
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{a,b,c}
is. As mentioned in the beginning of Section
9
, the vertical shift in the definition of
\widetilde{U}
takes into account the height error of
N^{R\delta-1}
in the ceiling constraint on
(H_{t})
. Specifically, we have the following, which is the analog of the second statement of Lemma
8.9
.

Lemma 9.3
.

We have
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N-N^{R\delta-1}}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
for all
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
.

Proof.

By
3.18
we have

\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N}(x)-\mathcal{U}_{q}^{(j-1)/N}(x)\asymp\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j^{-1/3}N^{-2/3},

uniformly in
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
and in
j
. Since
k\gg N^{R\delta}
by (
9.4
), the level line
\mathcal{U}^{k/N-N^{R\delta-1}}_{q}
is at a distance at most of order

\sum_{j=k-N^{R\delta}}^{k}\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j^{-1/3}N^{-2/3}\asymp N^{R\delta-2/3}\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}k^{-1/3}

below
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{a,b,c}
. On the other hand, again by (
9.4
),

N^{R\delta-2/3}\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}k^{-1/3}\leq N^{-R\delta/3-2/3}\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}\ll\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+\delta/2}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}.

Therefore, the claim follows.
∎

The following is analogous to
Definition

8.8
and
Definition

8.10
(except, as mentioned in the beginning of Section
9
, we no longer require a Bernoulli path ensemble
\widecheck{U}
with its paths bounded above).

Definition 9.4
.

For
j\leq k-1
, let
y^{\pm,j}_{q}=\mathcal{U}^{j/N}_{q}(x^{\pm})
and
y^{\pm,j}_{a,b,c}=\widetilde{U}_{j}(x^{\pm})
.
Let
\widehat{U}=\{\widehat{U}_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq k-1}
denote the Bernoulli path ensemble with endpoints given by
13
13

13

As in
Definition

8.10
, the endpoints are points in
\mathbb{T}_{N}+(0,1/(2N))
at minimal distance from
\{(x^{\pm},y^{j,\pm}_{a,b,c})\}_{1\leq j\leq k-1}

\{(x^{\pm},y^{j,\pm}_{a,b,c})\}_{1\leq j\leq k-1}
, conditioned to the event that
\widehat{U}_{k-1}
is weakly below
\widetilde{U}_{k}
.

The following is analogous to the first statement of
8.9
.

Lemma 9.5
.

For
N^{R\delta}<j\leq k
, it holds
y^{\pm,j-N^{R\delta}}_{q}-y^{\pm,j}_{a,b,c}\gtrsim N^{2B\delta-1}{\omega\mathfrak{d}}
.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to that of
8.9
, so we just sketch it.
For
j=k
the claim readily follows from (
8.7
) and
(x^{+}-x^{-})^{2}\gtrsim L^{3}N^{2B\delta}\omega(qN)^{-1}
. To prove the claim for
j<k
, it is enough to show that

(9.6)

\displaystyle|(y^{\pm,k-N^{R\delta}}_{a,b,c}-y^{\pm,j-N^{R\delta}}_{a,b,c})-(y^{\pm,k}_{q}-y^{\pm,j}_{q})|\ll N^{2B\delta-1}{\omega\mathfrak{d}}.

Arguing like in (
8.11
),
we have thanks to (
3.22
) that

|(y^{\pm,k-N^{R\delta}}_{a,b,c}-y^{\pm,j-N^{R\delta}}_{a,b,c})-(y^{\pm,k}_{q}-y^{\pm,j}_{q})|\lesssim q\mathfrak{d}\left(\mathfrak{d}\frac{k}{N}\right)^{2/3}=\mathfrak{d}\omega\frac{q}{\omega^{3}}N^{20R\delta}\leq{\omega\mathfrak{d}}N^{-1+(20R+6A)\delta}

using (
9.1
) and the lower bound (
7.7
) on
\omega
. Because of (
7.20
), (
9.6
) follows.
∎

9.1.2.
Proof of
7.7
if
\mathfrak{d}=\bm{d}

The following lemma is analogous to
8.12
.

Lemma 9.6
.

W.o.p., the following holds:
\widehat{U}_{j}
is between
\widetilde{U}_{j-N^{3\delta/4}}
and
\widetilde{U}_{j+N^{3\delta/4}}
if
j-N^{3\delta/4}>0
and between
\widetilde{U}_{j+N^{3\delta/4}}
and
\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{1/N}-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+3\delta/4}
if
j
is smaller.

Proof.

The proof (based on
3.25
and on the second claim of
3.17
to address the case when
j\leq N^{3\delta/4}
) is almost identical to that of
8.12
and
8.26
, so we skip it.
∎

Proof of
7.7
if
\mathfrak{d}={\bm{d}}
.

Since the events in both claims of the proposition are decreasing, we can by monotonicity impose that above
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}
, the height function is frozen to the maximal configuration lower than the ceiling
\mathcal{H}_{q}+N^{R\delta-1}
. Since in the region below
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}
the height function is at least zero and at most
k/N\leq N^{30R\delta}/(\omega^{3}\mathfrak{d})\leq N^{33R\delta-1}
(where in the last inequality we used (
7.21
)),
2.13
implies that the mixing time satisfies
T_{\rm mix}^{\mathcal{Y}}\lesssim N^{1+5B\delta}\frac{\omega}{q}
(the argument is very similar to that in (
8.14
)).
Since
q^{-1}N^{1+10B\delta}\geq N^{\delta}T_{\rm mix}^{\mathcal{Y}}
, applying (
2.4
) and
2.14
, it is sufficient to prove that the two claims of the proposition hold, w.o.p., under the uniform measure
\pi
subject to the constraints that the height is lower than
\mathcal{H}_{q}+N^{R\delta-1}
, lower than
\mathcal{H}_{q}
on
\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{+}(R\delta)
and frozen in the region above
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}
to the maximal configuration smaller than
\mathcal{H}_{q}+N^{R\delta-1}
.

Call
W_{j}
the level line of the height function
H
in the cell

\mathcal{Y}
.
We claim that the Bernoulli path ensemble
\{W_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq k-1}
can be coupled with
\widehat{U}
in such a way that
W_{j}
is weakly above
\widehat{U}_{j}
for all
j
, almost surely. To see this, note that, by the ceiling constraint,
W_{j+N^{R\delta}}
is
deterministically above
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N}
; in particular,

W_{k}
is above
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N-N^{-1+R\delta}}
that is above

\widetilde{U}_{k}
by
9.3
. Denoting by
(x^{\pm},Y^{\pm,j})
the endpoints of the lines
W_{j},j\leq k
, we have for some positive constant
\kappa
that
Y^{\pm,j}\geq y^{\pm,j-N^{R\delta}}_{q}\geq y^{\pm,j}_{a,b,c}+\kappa N^{2B\delta-1}{\omega\mathfrak{d}}

for

j>N^{R\delta}
, by
9.5
.
On the other hand, again by the ceiling constraint,
W_{j}
is inside
\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{q}(R\delta)

for
j\leq N^{R\delta}
, that is,
W_{j}
is above

\mathcal{U}^{1/N}_{q}-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+R\delta}
, and therefore

(9.7)

\displaystyle Y^{\pm,j}\geq y^{\pm,1}_{q}-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+R\delta}\geq y^{\pm,N^{R\delta}}_{a,b,c}+\kappa N^{2B\delta-1}\omega\mathfrak{d}-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+R\delta}\geq y^{\pm,N^{R\delta}}_{a,b,c}\geq y^{\pm,j}_{a,b,c}

where we first applied
9.5
, then the
bound (
7.21
) on
\omega
and (
7.20
). Therefore, the endpoints of
W_{j}
are higher than those of
\widehat{U}_{j}
for all
j\leq k-1
.
The claimed domination of the Bernoulli ensemble
\widehat{U}
by
W
then follows from
2.12
.

To prove the first claim of the proposition it is then enough to show that,
w.o.p.,
\widehat{U}_{j_{\bar{z}}+N^{\delta}}(\bar{x})\geq\bar{y}
. By
9.6
,
\widehat{U}_{j_{\bar{z}}+N^{\delta}}

is w.o.p. above

\widetilde{U}_{j_{\bar{z}}+N^{\delta}-N^{3\delta/4}}
, therefore above

\widetilde{U}_{j_{\bar{z}}+N^{\delta}/2}=\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{j_{\bar{z}}/N+N^{\delta-1}/2}-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+\delta/2}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}
.
On the other hand, by

8.13
,
\mathcal{U}^{j/N}_{a,b,c}(\bar{x})\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N}(\bar{x})
for
j\leq k
.
Wrapping up, we have
\widehat{U}_{j_{\bar{z}}+N^{\delta}}(\bar{x})\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j_{\bar{z}}/N+N^{\delta-1}/2}(\bar{x})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+\delta/2}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}

and it remains to show that

(9.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j_{\bar{z}}/N+N^{\delta-1}/2}(\bar{x})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+\delta/2}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}\geq\bar{y}=\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j_{\bar{z}}/N}(\bar{x}).

On the other hand, the third statement of
3.18
(see also (
3.10
)) implies

(9.9)

\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j_{\bar{z}}/N+N^{\delta-1}/2}(\bar{x})-\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j_{\bar{z}}/N}(\bar{x})\gtrsim\sum_{j=j_{\bar{z}}}^{j_{\bar{z}}+N^{\delta}/2}\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3}}{j^{1/3}}\asymp\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3}(N^{2\delta/3}{\bf 1}_{j_{\bar{z}}\leq N^{\delta}}+\frac{N^{\delta}}{j_{\bar{z}}^{1/3}}{\bf 1}_{j_{\bar{z}}\geq N^{\delta}})\\
\gtrsim\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3}}{j_{\bar{z}}^{1/3}}N^{(2/3)\delta}\gg\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+\delta/2}}{j_{\bar{z}}^{1/3}}.

Item

1
is proven.

Next, we prove
Item

2
of
7.7
and we take

\bar{z}\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}\setminus\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{+}(\delta)
(the
condition
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\in(L^{-2}\mathfrak{d},L^{2}\mathfrak{d})
is automatically
satisfied in the case
\mathfrak{d}={\bm{d}}
if
L
is large, thanks to

8.2
and to (
7.6
), that guarantees that

\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}=\mathfrak{d}_{\bar{z}}
). The goal is to prove that, w.o.p. under the measure
\pi
,

W_{1}
is above
\bar{z}
(that is,
H(\bar{z})=\mathcal{H}_{q}(\bar{z})=0
). We can assume that

\bar{z}\in\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{+}(R\delta)
since outside of

\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{+}(R\delta)
the height
H
is zero, because of
the ceiling constraint
H\leq\mathcal{H}_{q}=0
.
Since
W
dominates
\widehat{U}
, it suffices to show that
\widehat{U}_{1}(\bar{x})\geq y
, w.o.p.; by
9.6
and
8.13
,
\widehat{U}_{1}(\bar{x})\geq\mathcal{U}^{1/N}_{a,b,c}(\bar{x})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+3\delta/4}\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{1/N}(\bar{x})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+3\delta/4}
. The
claim then follows since
\bar{z}\not\in\mathfrak{L}^{+}_{q}(\delta)
implies that
\bar{y}\leq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{1/N}(\bar{x})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{-2/3+4\delta/5}
.
∎

9.2.
Case II:
\bm{d}>\mathfrak{d}

In this section we prove
7.7
when
\bm{d}>\mathfrak{d}
. The
proof will be quite similar to that of
7.7
when

\mathfrak{d}=\bm{d}
, and to that of
7.6
when
\bm{d}>\mathfrak{d}
. In fact, it will admit some simplifications
with respect to the latter; specifically, since the number of curves

k\leq N^{33R\delta}
(as noted after (
9.1
)) is reasonably small, we will be able to
circumvent the inductive procedure implemented there, by instead
shifting the curve
\widetilde{U}
slightly to the right (see
Definition
9.12
below), in addition to down (as in
(
9.5
)). We will also not require the point

\mathfrak{z}
from
Definition

8.19
, as we will not need to make use of a
“transition” between the two different ceiling constraints.

9.2.1.
Properties of cells and the
k
-th level line

Recall from the beginning of Section
9
that we have fixed
\bar{z}=(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}
. By symmetry (see also
8.1
), we may assume that
\bar{x}\geq x_{q}^{SW}
(that is,
\bar{z}
is to the right of
p^{SW}_{q}
). In analogy with Definition
8.15
, we define
x^{\pm}
as follows.

Definition 9.7
.

Recalling the definition of
k
from (
9.1
), set

\displaystyle x^{\pm}=\bar{x}\pm N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}

and let
{\bf L}^{\pm}
denote the line parallel to the
y
-axis and with
x
-coordinate
x^{\pm}
.

Remark 9.8
.

Since
\bar{z}\in\mathcal{Y}^{-}
, it is quickly seen from (
7.12
) and the fact that
\bm{d}\geq L^{-2}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}
, together with the definition (
7.13
) of the cell
\mathcal{Y}
, that the vertical lines
\mathbf{L}^{\pm}
intersect the cell
\mathcal{Y}.
Also observe by (
9.1
), (
7.7
) and (
7.20
) (and that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\in(L^{-2}\mathfrak{d},L^{2}\mathfrak{d})
) that

(9.10)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{k}{N}\lesssim N^{(30R+6A)\delta}\mathfrak{d}^{2}\ll N^{36R\delta}\mathfrak{d}^{2}.

Remark 9.9
.

We can assume that

(9.11)

\displaystyle(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q})^{1/2}\leq N^{R\delta}\omega^{-1}.

Indeed, for
Item

1
this is an assumption of the proposition, and for
Item

2
,
(\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q})^{1/2}\asymp\mathfrak{d}^{1/3}N^{-1/3}
(because
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}=\mathfrak{d}_{\bar{z}}\asymp\mathfrak{d}
, the latter thanks to the assumption
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\in(L^{-2}\mathfrak{d},L^{2}\mathfrak{d})
, and
\mathtt{e}_{\bar{z};q}=\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3}\asymp\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3}
, the first equality in the latter formula being true by
Definition

3.12
because
\bar{z}
is outside the liquid region), while
N^{R\delta}\omega^{-1}\geq\mathfrak{d}^{1/3}N^{(R+A)\delta-1/3}
by (
7.7
).
Then, as in
9.1
, it follows that

(9.12)

\displaystyle k\geq N^{8R\delta}j_{\bar{z}}

with
j_{\bar{z}}
defined in (
9.2
).

The following is the analog of
9.2
:

Lemma 9.10
.

The graph of the curve
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]\mapsto\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)
is contained in
\mathcal{Y}
.

Proof.

Letting
z=(x,y)\in\mathcal{Y}
denote any point on the top boundary of
\mathcal{Y}
with
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)\geq kN^{-1}
.
Since the top boundary of
\mathcal{Y}
is horizontal and has vertical coordinate
L^{5}N^{2B\delta}{\bm{d}}^{2}
(in the case
\mathfrak{d}<{\bm{d}}
) and the height is decreasing in the horizontal direction, it is enough to show the inequality for
x=x^{+},y=L^{5}N^{2B\delta}{\bm{d}}^{2}
.
Observe from
Definition

9.7
that
x^{+}-x_{q}^{SW}\lesssim N^{B\delta/2}\mathfrak{d}_{\bar{z}}
and
y\gg(x^{+}-x_{q}^{SW})^{2}
(as
\mathfrak{d}_{\bar{z}}\leq L^{2}\mathfrak{d}
). Hence, since there exist constants
c>0
and
C>1
for which
\mathfrak{e}_{z;q}\geq cy^{1/2}-C(x^{+}-x_{q}^{SW})
(because the arctic boundary has positive and finite curvature, by
3.10
), it follows that
\mathfrak{e}_{z;q}\gtrsim y^{1/2}=\mathfrak{d}_{z}
. Thus, by
3.18
,

\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)\gtrsim\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}=y=L^{5}N^{2B\delta}{\bm{d}}^{2}
.
Since
k/N=N^{30R\delta}(\omega^{3}\mathfrak{d})^{-1}\leq N^{36R\delta}\mathfrak{d}^{2}\leq N^{36R\delta}{\bm{d}}^{2}
(by (
7.7
) and the assumption
\mathfrak{d}<{\bm{d}}
), the claim
\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)\geq k/N
follows from (
7.20
).
∎

Lemma 9.11
.

If
z_{x}=(x,\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x))
and
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
, then
\mathfrak{d}_{z_{x}}\leq N^{B\delta/2}\mathfrak{d}
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}
intersects the arctic boundary
\mathfrak{A}_{q}
at a point
\mathfrak{w}_{0}=(x_{0},k/N)
with
x_{0}\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
, and
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)=k/N
for
x\leq x_{0}
.

Proof.

The implicit constants in the bounds
\lesssim,\asymp
below can depend on
L
.
Note that
x\mapsto U^{k/N}(x)
is increasing, so it is enough to prove the first statement of the lemma for

x=x^{+}
. Observe that
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{0}(x_{+})\lesssim(x^{+}-x_{q}^{SW})^{2}\lesssim N^{B\delta}\mathfrak{d}^{2}
by the fact that
x^{+}-\bar{x}\lesssim N^{B\delta/2}\mathfrak{d}
, that
\bar{x}-x_{q}^{SW}\lesssim\mathfrak{d}
and that the curvature of the arctic boundary is bounded. Hence, for some constant
C>1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{z_{x^{+}}}^{2}=\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x_{+})\leq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x^{+})-\mathcal{U}_{q}^{0}(x^{+})+CN^{B\delta}\mathfrak{d}^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{d}_{z_{x^{+}}}^{2/3}(kN^{-1})^{2/3}+N^{B\delta}\mathfrak{d}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{d}_{z_{x^{+}}}^{2/3}N^{24R\delta-2/3}+N^{B\delta}\mathfrak{d}^{2},

where the first statement follows from the definition of
z_{x_{+}}
; the second from the above bound on
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{0}(x^{+})
; the third from
3.18
; and the fourth from the definition of
k
, with (
7.21
). This implies that either
\mathfrak{d}_{z_{x^{+}}}\lesssim N^{B\delta/2}\mathfrak{d}
or that
\mathfrak{d}_{z_{x^{+}}}\lesssim N^{18R\delta-1/2}\lesssim N^{18R\delta}\mathfrak{d}
by (
7.6
). So, by (
7.20
) we have either way that
\mathfrak{d}_{z_{x^{+}}}\leq N^{B\delta/2}\mathfrak{d}
.

Since the arctic boundary is uniformly convex, the point
\mathfrak{w}_{0}=(x_{0},k/N)
satisfies
x^{SW}_{q}-x_{0}\asymp\sqrt{k/N}\lesssim N^{18R\delta}\mathfrak{d}
, by (
9.10
). Since
\bar{x}-x^{SW}_{q}\lesssim\mathfrak{d}
and
\bar{x}-x^{-}\asymp N^{B\delta/9}\mathfrak{d}\gg N^{18R\delta}\mathfrak{d}
by (
7.20
), it follows that
x_{0}\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
.
Finally, the fact that
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}(x)=k/N
for
x\in[x^{-},x_{0}]
follows from
3.23
.
∎

9.2.2.
Comparison of level lines

Now let
s_{x}=x-\mathfrak{l}_{q}(k/N)
, and denote

\displaystyle\breve{x}=\bar{x}-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta};\qquad s_{0}=s_{\breve{x}}=s_{\bar{x}}-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta};

\displaystyle s_{+}=s_{x^{+}};\qquad s_{-}=0.

By
3.26
(as in
8.17
, one checks that the assumptions of the proposition are satisfied with the above choice of
s_{0},s_{\pm}
),
we can find
a,b,c\in[1-C\varepsilon_{0},1+C\varepsilon_{0}]
(once again, set
\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}
from those formulas to
0
, for lightness of notation) such that

(9.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}(\breve{x})=\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{a,b,c}(\breve{x}),\quad\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}(\breve{x})=\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{a,b,c}(\breve{x});\\
&\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)-\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(\breve{x})\geq\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{k/N}(x)-\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{k/N}(\breve{x})+C^{-1}q\mathfrak{d}(x-\breve{x})^{2}\quad\forall x\in[\breve{x},x^{+}];\\
&\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{k/N}(x)\quad\forall x\in[x^{-},x^{+}].\end{aligned}

Definition 9.12
.

Define
\widetilde{U}_{j}
by shifting
\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{j/N}
(vertically) down by
\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{\delta/2-2/3}
and (horizontally) to the right by
\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}
. Specifically, for
j\leq k
and
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
, set

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{j}(x)=\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{j/N}(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{\delta/2-2/3}.

Furthermore, for each
t\in[0,N^{5}]
, let
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x)
denote the
jN^{-1}
-level line (as in Definition
2.6
, recalling also
Footnote

11
) of
H_{t}
, and set
\mathtt{y}_{t}^{\pm,j}=\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x^{\pm})
.

The following two lemmas provide the analog of
8.23
(which will also replace
9.3
and
9.5
) in our setting. The vertical shift of
\widetilde{U}_{j}
in
Definition

9.12
will facilitate in ensuring in Lemma
9.13
that
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
for
x\leq\breve{x}
; the horizontal shift will facilitate in ensuring that
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{1/N}(x^{+})\geq\widetilde{U}_{1}(x^{+})
in Lemma
9.14
.

Lemma 9.13
.

We have w.o.p. that
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{k}(x)
, for all
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
and
t\in[0,N^{5}]
.

Proof.

By Definition
9.12
and the last bound in (
9.13
), it suffices to show w.o.p. that
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{\delta/2-2/3}
for all
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
and
t\in[0,N^{5}]
. We may assume below that

(9.14)

\displaystyle(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta},\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}))\notin\mathfrak{F}_{q}^{SW},

for otherwise
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta})
takes the minimal value
k/N
and therefore
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta})
holds.

Next, by the ceiling constraint on
H_{t}
, we have
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N-N^{R\delta-1}}(x)
, so it remains to show

(9.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N-N^{R\delta-1}}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{\delta/2-2/3}.

To that end, first observe for any
h\geq 0
that by
Definition

3.22
we have
\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}_{q}^{h}(x)=-\partial_{x}\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)\cdot\partial_{y}\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)^{-1}
, where
z=(x,\mathcal{U}_{q}^{h}(x))
. Thus,

(9.16)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}_{q}^{h}(x)\asymp\mathfrak{d}_{z},\quad\text{if $x\geq x_{q}^{SW}$};\qquad\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}_{q}^{h}(x)\asymp(\mathfrak{d}_{z}\mathfrak{e}_{z;q})^{1/2},\quad\text{if $x\leq x_{q}^{SW}$}.

where the first statement follows from
3.18
, and the second follows from the facts (see the fourth part of
3.18
) that
-\partial_{x}\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)\asymp(\mathfrak{d}_{z}\mathfrak{e}_{z;q})^{1/2}
and
\partial_{y}\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)\asymp 1
for
x\leq x_{q}^{SW}
. We additionally have for any
x\in[x_{-},x_{+}]
that, denoting
z=(x,\mathcal{U}_{q}^{h}(x))
,

(9.17)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{z}\asymp|x-x_{q}^{SW}|+h^{1/2},

where this holds for
x\leq x_{q}^{SW}
since then
h=\mathcal{H}_{q}(z)\asymp\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}
(and
|x-x_{q}^{SW}|=x_{q}^{SW}-x\lesssim h^{1/2}
in this case) and it holds for
x\geq x_{q}^{SW}
by (
9.16
) (with the fact that
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{h}(x)=\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{2}
).

We next use (
9.16
) and (
9.17
) to show (
9.15
); denote
z_{0}=(x,\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x))\in\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}
. First assume that
x\geq x_{q}^{SW}
. Then, for a constant
c>0
, we have

(9.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta})\leq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)-c\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}.

since
\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x^{\prime})\gtrsim\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}
for
x^{\prime}\in[x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}N^{-2/3-\delta},x]
. To verify the latter bound, observe it holds at
x^{\prime}=x
, by (
9.16
); for other
x^{\prime}
, we have denoting
z^{\prime}=(x^{\prime},\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x^{\prime}))
that
\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x^{\prime})\gtrsim\mathfrak{d}_{z^{\prime}}
, by (
9.16
). So, we must verify that
\mathfrak{d}_{z^{\prime}}\gtrsim\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}
. If this were false then, since
x-x^{\prime}\leq\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}\ll N^{-\delta}\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}
(as
\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}\geq N^{-1/2}),

\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}
would have decreased too quickly at some point in
[x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta},x]
. Specifically, we would have that
\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}_{k/N}(x^{\prime\prime})\gg\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}
for some
x^{\prime\prime}\in[x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta},x]
, which contradicts (
9.16
). Thus
\partial_{x}\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x^{\prime})\gtrsim\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}
, and hence (
9.18
), holds. Moreover, for any
j\in[k-N^{R\delta},k]
, we have by
3.18
, (
9.12
) and (
3.10
) that

(9.19)

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N}(x)-\mathcal{U}_{q}^{(j-1)/N}(x)\lesssim\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}^{2/3}j^{-1/3}N^{-2/3}.

Therefore, since
j\geq k/2
,

(9.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)-\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N-N^{R\delta-1}}(x)\lesssim\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}^{2/3}k^{-1/3}N^{R\delta-2/3}.

By (
9.18
) and (
9.20
), to confirm (
9.15
) when
x\geq x_{q}^{SW}
, it suffices to verify that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}^{2/3}k^{-1/3}N^{R\delta-2/3}\ll\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}+\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{\delta/2-2/3},

which holds since
k\geq N^{4R\delta}j_{\bar{z}}
by (
9.4
) (together with the fact that
a+b\geq(a^{2}b/4)^{1/3}
for any real numbers
a,b\geq 0
).

Now assume instead that
x\in[x^{-},x_{q}^{SW}]
is to the left of the southwest tangency location. Note that (
9.16
) and (
9.17
) (together with
\mathfrak{d}_{z}\gtrsim\mathfrak{d}_{z}^{1/2}\mathfrak{e}_{z;q}^{1/2}
) give for a constant
c>0
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta})\leq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)-c(kN^{-1})^{1/4}\mathfrak{e}_{z_{0};q}^{1/2}\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}.

Moreover, since
\partial_{y}\mathcal{H}_{q}(x,\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N}(x))\asymp 1
for
x\leq x_{q}^{SW}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x)-\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N-N^{R\delta-1}}(x)\asymp N^{R\delta-1}.

Hence, to confirm (
9.15
) when
x\leq x_{q}^{SW}
, it suffices to verify that

(9.21)

\displaystyle N^{R\delta-1}\ll(kN^{-1})^{1/4}\mathfrak{e}_{z_{0};q}^{1/2}\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}+\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{\delta/2-2/3}.

Due to the second term on the right side of (
9.21
), this bound holds if
\mathfrak{d}\geq j_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}N^{3R\delta/2-1/2}
. So, assume instead that
\mathfrak{d}<j_{\bar{z}}^{1/2}N^{3R\delta/2-1/2}
. In this case, we use the fact that
\mathfrak{e}_{z_{0};q}\geq\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}\geq j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/6}N^{-(R/2+1)\delta-1/2}
, where the first bound follows from (
9.14
), and so (
9.21
) becomes equivalent to

\displaystyle N^{R\delta-1}\ll k^{1/4}\cdot j_{\bar{z}}^{-7/12}N^{-1-3R\delta/4-3\delta/2}.

Therefore, it suffices to verify that
k^{1/4}j_{\bar{z}}^{-7/12}\gg N^{2R\delta}
.
By (
9.3
) and (
7.21
), we have that
j_{\bar{z}}\lesssim N^{3R\delta}\cdot N/(\omega^{3}\mathfrak{d})\leq N^{6R\delta}
, so that
j_{\bar{z}}^{7/12}\leq N^{7R\delta/2}
. Thus, it suffices to verify that
k\gg N^{22R\delta}
, which holds by (
9.1
) and (
7.7
).
This establishes the lemma.
∎

Lemma 9.14
.

For both
x\in\{x^{-},x^{+}\}
, we have w.o.p. that
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x)\geq\widetilde{U}_{j}(x)
, for all
j\in[1,k-1]
and
t\in[0,N^{5}]
.

Proof.

Observe for all
j\in[1,k]
that
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x^{-})\geq\widetilde{U}_{j}(x^{-})
, since
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x^{-})\geq\widetilde{U}_{k}(x^{-})
(by Lemma
9.13
) and
\widetilde{U}_{k}(x^{-})-\widetilde{U}_{j}(x^{-})=(k-j)N^{-1}\leq\mathtt{U}_{t}^{k/N}(x^{-})-\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x^{-})
(the first since
(x^{-},\widetilde{U}_{k}(x^{-}))\in\mathfrak{F}_{a,b,c}^{SW}
, and the second since the
y-
derivative of the height is at most
1
). It therefore remains to show that
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x^{+})\geq\widetilde{U}_{j}(x^{+})
holds w.o.p. for all
j\in[1,k]
.

To that end, set
z_{0}=(x^{+},\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x^{+}))\in\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}
and let
j\in[1,k]
. Then, w.o.p., we have for some constant
C>1
that

\displaystyle\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x^{+})-\widetilde{U}_{j}(x^{+})

\displaystyle\geq\max\{\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N-N^{R\delta-1}}(x^{+}),\mathcal{U}_{q}^{1/N}(x^{+})-\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}^{2/3}N^{R\delta-2/3}\}-\widetilde{U}_{k}(x^{+})

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x^{+})-Ck^{2/3}\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}^{2/3}N^{R\delta-2/3}-\widetilde{U}_{k}(x^{+})

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x^{+})-\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}(x^{+}-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta})-Ck^{2/3}\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}^{2/3}N^{R\delta-2/3},

where the first statement follows from the ceiling constraints (
7.22
) and (
7.23
) on
(H_{t})
; the second follows from summing (
9.19
) over
j\in[1,k]
; and the third follows from Definition
9.12
with the third statement of (
9.13
). Thus, by (
9.18
), we have w.o.p. for some constant
c>0
that

(9.22)

\displaystyle\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x^{+})-\widetilde{U}_{j}(x^{+})

\displaystyle\geq c\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}-Ck^{2/3}\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}^{2/3}N^{R\delta-2/3}.

Further observe that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}\cdot(k^{2/3}\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}^{2/3}N^{R\delta-2/3})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{d}_{z_{0}}^{1/3}\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}\cdot j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}k^{-2/3}N^{-(R+1)\delta}

\displaystyle\geq N^{B\delta/30}N^{-33R\delta}\gg 1,

where the second statement follows from (
9.17
), (
9.4
), the fact that
x^{+}-x_{q}^{SW}\geq x^{+}-\bar{x}=N^{B\delta/9}\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq N^{B\delta/10}\mathfrak{d}
(by Definition
9.7
and the fact that
\mathtt{d}_{\bar{z}}\geq L^{-2}\mathfrak{d}
), (
9.4
), and the fact from (
9.1
) and (
7.21
) that
k\leq N^{33R\delta}
; the third follows from (
7.20
). This, together with (
9.22
) yields w.o.p.
\mathtt{U}_{t}^{j/N}(x^{+})\geq\widetilde{U}_{j}(x^{+})
, which shows the lemma. ∎

9.2.3.
Proof of
7.7
when
\mathfrak{d}<\bm{d}

The following is the analog of
Definition

9.4
.

Definition 9.15
.

For
j\leq k-1
, set
y^{\pm,j}_{a,b,c}=\widetilde{U}_{j}(x^{\pm})
. Let
\widehat{U}=\{\widehat{U}_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq k-1}
denote the Bernoulli path ensemble with endpoints given by
\{(x^{\pm},y^{j,\pm}_{a,b,c})\}_{1\leq j\leq k-1}
, conditioned to the event that
\widehat{U}_{k-1}
is weakly below
\widetilde{U}_{k}
.

The following is the analog of Lemma
9.6
in our setting, with a similar proof.

Lemma 9.16
.

W.o.p., the following holds for all
x\in[x^{-},x^{+}]
. For
j\in(N^{3\delta/4},k-1]
, we have
\widetilde{U}_{j-N^{3\delta/4}}(x)\leq\widehat{U}_{j}(x)\leq\widetilde{U}_{j+N^{3\delta/4}}(x)
. Moreover, for all
j\in[1,N^{3\delta/4}]
, we have that
\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{1/N}(x-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j^{-1/3}N^{-2/3+3\delta/4}\leq\widehat{U}_{j}(x)\leq\widetilde{U}_{j+N^{3\delta/4}}(x)
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
9.6
, the first statement of the lemma follows from
3.25
, and the second follows from the second claim of
3.17
.
∎

Proof of
7.7
if
\mathfrak{d}<{\bm{d}}
.

Given the above estimates, the proof of this proposition when
\bm{d}<\mathfrak{d}
is similar to that in the case
\mathfrak{d}=\bm{d}
provided in Section
9.1
; so, we only outline it here.

First, by
9.9
(in particular, (
9.12
)), we can restrict our attention to points
\bar{z}
satisfying
\mathcal{H}_{q}(\bar{z})\leq k/N
. Then, by monotonicity (
2.17
), we can freeze the height function above the line
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{k/N}
to the maximal configuration compatible with the ceiling constraints (
7.22
) and (
7.23
).

As in the proof of
7.7
when
\mathfrak{d}={\bm{d}}
, using
2.13
, we can quickly verify that the mixing time
T_{\rm mix}^{\mathcal{Y}}
in the cell
\mathcal{Y}
is at most
q^{-1}N^{1+5B\delta}\omega
. Therefore, in the time interval
[q^{-1}N^{1+10B\delta}\omega,N^{5}]
, we can couple height
(H_{t})
to coincide w.o.p. with the uniform measure
\pi
, subject to the constraints that the height is lower than
\mathcal{H}_{q}+N^{R\delta-1}
, lower than
\mathcal{H}_{q}
on
\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{+}(R\delta)
, and frozen in the region above
\mathcal{U}^{k/N}_{q}
to the maximal configuration smaller than
\mathcal{H}_{q}+N^{R\delta-1}
. Hence, by
2.14
, it suffices to show that both claims in
7.7
hold w.o.p. under this measure
\pi
.

By Lemma
9.13
and Lemma
9.14
, it suffices by monotonicity (
2.17
) to verify that both claims in
7.7
hold w.o.p. for the Bernoulli path ensemble
\widehat{U}
from Definition
9.15
. By Lemma
9.16
and Definition
9.12
, we have that

(9.23)

\displaystyle\widehat{U}_{j}(\bar{x})\geq\max\{\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{j/N-N^{3\delta/4-1}}(\breve{x}),\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{1/N}(\breve{x})\}-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}(j^{-1/3}+j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3})N^{3\delta/4-2/3}.

By (
3.21
) in Proposition
3.26
, together with the first statement in (
9.13
), we have
\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{j/N-N^{3\delta/4-1}}(\breve{x})\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N-N^{3\delta/4-1}}(\breve{x})
and
\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{1/N}(\breve{x})\geq\mathcal{U}_{q}^{1/N}(\breve{x})
. Setting
j=\max\{j_{\bar{z}}-1,1\}
, by these bounds with (
9.23
) (and recalling that
\breve{x}=\bar{x}-\mathfrak{d}^{-1/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3-\delta}
), it remains to show that

\displaystyle\max\{

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N-N^{3\delta/4-1}}(\breve{x}),\mathcal{U}_{q}^{1/N}(\breve{x})\}

\displaystyle\geq\max\{\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N-N^{\delta-1}}(\bar{x}),\mathcal{U}_{q}^{1/N}(\bar{x})-\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}N^{\delta-2/3}\}+3\mathfrak{d}^{2/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{3\delta/4-2/3},

(where
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{h}(\breve{x}):=-\infty
if
h<0
) which follows quickly from (
9.16
) and summing the bound
\mathcal{U}_{q}^{j/N}(\bar{x})-\mathcal{U}_{q}^{(j-1)/N}(\bar{x})\gtrsim\mathfrak{d}_{\bar{z}}^{2/3}j_{\bar{z}}^{-1/3}N^{-2/3}
(as a consequence of (
3.10
)) over
j\in[j_{\bar{z}}-N^{\delta},j_{\bar{z}}-N^{3\delta/4}]
. This confirms the proposition.
∎

Appendix A
Proof of height concentration estimate

A.1.
Proof of
3.17

In this section we establish
3.17
. Throughout this section, fix real numbers
R>1>\delta>0
and
a,b,c\in[R^{-1},R]
, as in
3.17
, and further let
H
denote the height function associated with a uniformly random tiling of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{a,b,c}
. We begin with the following lemma, originally due to

[
45
, Lemma 5.6]
(but that appears in the form stated
below as
[
33
, Equation (13)]
). It indicates the
first statement of
3.17
holds, which provides a
concentration bound for
H
everywhere in

{\mathfrak{X}}_{a,b,c}
.

Lemma A.1

(
[
33
, Equation (13)]
)
.

Item
1
of
3.17
holds.

It remains to verify the second statement of
3.17
, indicating that
H
is w.o.p. frozen outside of the augmented liquid region
\mathfrak{L}_{a,b,c}^{+}(\delta)
. To that end, set
K=\lfloor N^{\delta/3}\rfloor
and for each integer
i\in[1,bN]
(recall
b
is the vertical side-length of the hexagon) define the (random) level line
U_{i}(x)=\mathtt{U}_{a,b,c}^{(i-1/2)/N}(x)
of the tiling height function
H
; see
Definition

2.6
. Then, applying
A.1
(with the
\delta
in
3.17
equal to
\delta/4
here), we obtain w.o.p. that
U_{K}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{0}(x)
for all
x\in[0,a]
.

To show Item (2) of
3.17
, we must show that
U_{1}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{0}(x)-\mathtt{d}_{x}^{2/3}N^{\delta-2/3}
. To that end, abbreviate
\mathfrak{l}=\mathfrak{l}_{a,b,c}(0)
and, for any
x\geq\mathfrak{l}
, set
d_{x}=\max\{x-\mathfrak{l},N^{-1/2}\}\lesssim\mathtt{d}_{x}
. For each
j\in[1,K]
, define the event

\displaystyle\Omega_{j}=\bigcap_{x\in[\mathfrak{l},a]}\{U_{K-j+1}(x)\geq\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{0}(x)-(j-1)d_{x}^{2/3}N^{\delta/3-2/3}\},

which provides the above type of lower bound on
U_{K-j+1}
. As indicated above,
\Omega_{1}
holds w.o.p.; the following lemma with induction will indicate that
\Omega_{K}
holds w.o.p. as well.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
j\in[1,K-1]
be an integer. If
\Omega_{j}
holds w.o.p., then
\Omega_{j+1}
holds w.o.p., as well.

Proof of
3.17
, Item (2).

By rotating
\mathfrak{X}_{a,b,c}
and permuting
(a,b,c)
if necessary, we may assume that
z=(x,y)
lies in the connected component of
\mathfrak{X}\setminus\mathfrak{L}_{a,b,c}^{+}(\delta)
containing the south corner
(a,0)
of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{a,b,c}
. By symmetry, we may further assume that the closest side of
\partial\mathfrak{X}_{a,b,c}
to
z
is its SW one, connecting
(0,0)
to
(a,0)
; hence,
x\in[\mathfrak{l},a]
. It suffices to show that
H(z)=0
, or equivalently that
U_{1}(x)>y
. Since
y\leq\mathcal{U}_{a,b,c}^{0}(x)-\mathfrak{d}_{x}^{2/3}N^{\delta-2/3}
, it remains to show (as
\mathtt{d}_{x}\lesssim\mathfrak{d}_{x}
) that
