\theta=\theta({\hat{x}},h^{\prime}):=\pi\left.\partial_{y}\mathtt{H}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}({\hat{x}},{\hat{y}})\right|_{{\hat{y}}=U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}})}=\frac{\pi}{\partial_{h}U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}})}\in(0,\pi).

From (
C.147
) we have
S({\hat{x}},U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}}))=\sqrt{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}{\hat{x}}\tan(\theta)
and therefore

(C.154)

\displaystyle U_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}({\hat{x}})=-\frac{\partial_{x}\mathtt{H}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}({\hat{x}},U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}}))}{\partial_{y}\mathtt{H}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}({\hat{x}},U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}}))}=\sqrt{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}\frac{S({\hat{x}},U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}}))}{\theta}=\gamma^{\prime\prime}{\hat{x}}\,\frac{\tan\theta}{\theta}.

Substituting
\sqrt{2U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}})-\gamma^{\prime\prime}{\hat{x}}^{2}}=\sqrt{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}{\hat{x}}\tan\theta
into the identity

\mathtt{H}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}({\hat{x}},U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}}))=h^{\prime}
yields

\displaystyle h^{\prime}=\frac{\gamma^{\prime\prime}{\hat{x}}^{2}}{2\pi}\,f(\theta),\qquad f(\theta)=\frac{\theta-\sin\theta\cos\theta}{\cos^{2}\theta}.

Since
f^{\prime}(\theta)=\frac{2\theta\sin\theta}{\cos^{3}\theta}>0
, it follows that
\theta({\hat{x}},h^{\prime})
is strictly decreasing as
{\hat{x}}
increases. Therefore

\partial_{h}U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}})=\pi/\theta({\hat{x}},h^{\prime})
is strictly increasing in
{\hat{x}}
.
Let us also show that
U_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}({\hat{x}})
is strictly increasing in
{\hat{x}}
. Using

{\hat{x}}=\sqrt{2\pi h^{\prime}/[f(\theta)\gamma^{\prime\prime}]}
, we may rewrite

(C.155)

\displaystyle U_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}({\hat{x}})=\sqrt{2\pi h^{\prime}/\gamma^{\prime\prime}}\,\psi(\theta),\qquad\psi(\theta):=\frac{\tan\theta}{\theta\sqrt{f(\theta)}}.

A direct computation shows that
\psi^{\prime}(\theta)<0
, so
U_{h^{\prime}}^{\prime}({\hat{x}})
is strictly decreasing as a function of

\theta
, hence strictly increasing as a function of
{\hat{x}}
.
Differentiating
\widetilde{U}_{h}(t)=U_{h}(\Phi_{M,Q}(t))
with respect to
t
and
evaluating at
t={\hat{x}}_{0}
gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{h}^{\prime}({\hat{x}}_{0})

\displaystyle=U_{h}^{\prime}(\Phi)\bigl(1+Q(M+2{\hat{x}}_{0})\bigr)=(1-Q\eta)U_{h}^{\prime}(\Phi).

Hence, by the defining property of
{\hat{x}}_{1}={\hat{x}}_{1}^{[-2{\hat{x}}_{0}-\eta]}
,

(C.156)

\displaystyle U_{h}^{\prime}({\hat{x}}_{1})=(1-Q\eta)U_{h}^{\prime}(\Phi)<U_{h}^{\prime}(\Phi).

Note that
U_{h}^{\prime}(\Phi)\asymp 1
, thanks to (
C.155
) and the fact that
\theta=\pi\partial_{y}\mathtt{H}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
is bounded away from zero, which follows from (
C.147
) together with the fact that the
S({\hat{x}},U_{h}({\hat{x}}))
is bounded away from zero if
h
is.
Since
U_{h}^{\prime}
is strictly increasing on
(0,\infty)
and
U_{h}^{\prime}(\Phi)\asymp 1
, (
C.156
)
implies
\Phi-{\hat{x}}_{1}^{[-2{\hat{x}}_{0}-\eta]}\asymp\eta Q
. Hence, since
\partial_{x}\partial_{h}U_{h}\gtrsim 1
as shown above,

\displaystyle\partial_{h}U_{h^{\prime}}(\Phi)-\partial_{h}U_{h^{\prime}}({\hat{x}}_{1}^{[-2{\hat{x}}_{0}-\eta]})\gtrsim(\Phi-{\hat{x}}_{1}^{[-2{\hat{x}}_{0}-\eta]})\gtrsim\eta Q,

which shows (
C.140
). The claim
\inf\{c(h^{\prime},h,{\hat{x}}_{0}):h^{\prime}\in[\varepsilon,h]\}>0
follows from the fact that for
h^{\prime}
bounded away from zero, the argument of the square root in the definition of
\mathtt{H}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
is also bounded away from zero.
∎

Proof of
C.33
.

We will prove the statement with
\eta=0
. The claim then follows by continuity (indeed, the presence of
\eta
cannot produce a linear term in
w
in the r.h.s. of (
C.141
), since the derivatives of
U_{h;M,Q}
and
U_{h;0;0}
at
{\hat{x}}_{1}
and
{\hat{x}}_{0}
coincide). The assumption
{\hat{x}}_{0}+w>\delta-\sqrt{2h/\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
(and therefore also
{\hat{x}}_{1}+w>\delta-\sqrt{2h/\gamma^{\prime\prime}}+O(Q)
) implies that the function
U_{h}
in the proof is computed at values of
{\hat{x}}
where it is smooth.
Write

\displaystyle U(t):=U_{h;0,0}(t),\qquad\widetilde{U}(t):=U_{h;M,Q}(t),\qquad\Phi(t):=\Phi_{M,Q}(t).

By (
C.151
), we have
{\hat{x}}_{1}={\hat{x}}_{0}-Q{\hat{x}}_{0}^{2}=\Phi({\hat{x}}_{0})
. Since
\widetilde{U}(t)=U(\Phi(t))
, we have
\widetilde{U}({\hat{x}}_{0})=U({\hat{x}}_{1})
. At the shifted point
{\hat{x}}^{\prime}={\hat{x}}_{0}+w
,

\displaystyle\Phi({\hat{x}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle={\hat{x}}^{\prime}+Q\bigl(M{\hat{x}}^{\prime}+({\hat{x}}^{\prime})^{2}\bigr)={\hat{x}}_{0}+w-Q{\hat{x}}_{0}^{2}+Qw^{2}={\hat{x}}_{1}+w+Qw^{2}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}({\hat{x}}^{\prime})=U({\hat{x}}_{1}+w+Qw^{2}).

Subtracting the unperturbed shifted difference gives

\displaystyle\bigl(\widetilde{U}({\hat{x}}^{\prime})-\widetilde{U}({\hat{x}}_{0})\bigr)-\bigl(U({\hat{x}}_{1}+w)-U({\hat{x}}_{1})\bigr)=U({\hat{x}}_{1}+w+Qw^{2})-U({\hat{x}}_{1}+w).

Because
{\hat{x}}^{\prime}>-\sqrt{2h/\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
and
{\hat{x}}_{1}+w={\hat{x}}^{\prime}+\mathcal{O}(Q)
, the points
{\hat{x}}_{1}+w
and

{\hat{x}}_{1}+w+Qw^{2}
remain in a compact subinterval of
(-\sqrt{2h/\gamma^{\prime\prime}},\infty)
for all
sufficiently small
Q
.
Taylor expansion at
{\hat{x}}_{1}+w
yields

\displaystyle U({\hat{x}}_{1}+w+Qw^{2})-U({\hat{x}}_{1}+w)

\displaystyle=Qw^{2}U^{\prime}({\hat{x}}_{1}+w)+\mathcal{O}(Q^{2}).

As proven above,
U^{\prime}
is positive and strictly increasing in
{\hat{x}}
. Moreover,
U^{\prime}(-\sqrt{2h/\gamma^{\prime\prime}}+\delta)\gtrsim\delta^{1/2}
holds by (
C.154
), since
\theta\gtrsim 1
(as for
\delta\ll 1
we have
|\theta-\pi|\ll 1
) and
S(\hat{x},U_{h}(\hat{x}))\asymp\delta^{1/2}
, as is easily seen from the definition
S(\hat{x},\hat{y})=\sqrt{2\hat{y}-\gamma^{\prime\prime}\hat{x}^{2}}
. This implies (
C.141
).
∎
